   27: # File extensions that Database::Abstraction can probe, in probe order.
   28: # .sql, .sqlite, and .sqlite3 are all SQLite databases; .db is Berkeley DB.
   29: Readonly my @SUPPORTED_EXT => qw( csv db sql sqlite sqlite3 xml tsv psv xlsx );
   30: # Use \z (absolute end-of-string) not $ (which permits a trailing \n before \z).
   31: # A query param decoded from "file.csv%0A" has basename "sales.csv\n"; without \z
   32: # that passes the extension guard and reaches realpath with an embedded newline.
   33: Readonly my $EXT_RE        => do { my $p = join '|', @SUPPORTED_EXT; qr/\.(?:$p)\z/i };
   34: 
   35: # Table name safe-identifier pattern -- must match DataSource's TABLE_NAME_RE
   36: # exactly: first character must be a letter or underscore (not a digit), so
   37: # that every table name the controller accepts can also be opened by DataSource
   38: # without an unhandled croak.

   40: 
   41: # URL spec pattern shared by _open_spec and _spec_to_url.
   42: # qr{} delimiter avoids the \/ escaping noise required by the // form.
   43: # /s: makes . match \n so a percent-decoded newline inside a URL does not
   44: # silently truncate the captured URL before the \z end-of-string anchor.
   45: Readonly my $URL_SPEC_RE   => qr{\Aurl:(https?://.+)\z}si;
   46: 
   47: # Redis spec pattern: redis:tablename|redis://[password@]host[:port][/db_index]

   49: # colons and slashes do not require extra quoting.

  163: # _is_safe_url($url) -> bool
  164: #
  165: # Purpose: SSRF guard.  Blocks the most dangerous classes of Server-Side Request
  166: #          Forgery targets: loopback aliases (localhost, 127/8) and literal
  167: #          private/link-local/CGNAT IPv4 addresses in the URL host component.
  168: #
  169: # Design rationale for scope:
  170: #   Resolving hostnames via DNS and checking the result is ineffective: a
  171: #   separate DNS lookup happens at LWP connect time (TOCTOU / DNS rebinding
  172: #   window).  The authoritative defence against hostname-based SSRF is a
  173: #   network-layer egress firewall.  This function handles the Perl-layer
  174: #   interception for the most common patterns that operators cannot easily
  175: #   filter at the network level: bare loopback aliases and literal private IPs
  176: #   hard-coded by an attacker.
  177: #
  178: # Blocked targets:
  179: #   localhost / 127.0.0.0/8 / 0.0.0.0 / ::1  -- loopback aliases
  180: #   10.0.0.0/8    -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  181: #   172.16.0.0/12 -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  182: #   192.168.0.0/16-- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  183: #   169.254.0.0/16-- link-local; AWS/GCP/Azure metadata endpoint (literal IP)
  184: #   100.64.0.0/10 -- CGNAT / Tailscale shared space (literal IP)

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  185: #
  186: # Hostname-based targets (e.g. http://internal.corp.example.com/) are allowed
  187: # at this layer; block them with egress firewall rules instead.
  188: sub _is_safe_url {
  189: 	my $url = $_[0];
  190: 
  191: 	return 0 unless $url =~ m{\Ahttps?://([^/:?\[\]#]+)}i;
  192: 	my $host = lc $1;

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  193: 
  194: 	# Block well-known loopback aliases.
  195: 	return 0 if $host eq 'localhost'
  196: 	          || $host =~ /\A127\./
  197: 	          || $host eq '0.0.0.0'

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  198: 	          || $host eq '::1';

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  199: 

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  200: 	# For literal IPv4 addresses only: check private/link-local/CGNAT ranges.

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  201: 	# Skipping DNS for hostname targets avoids a live network call in tests and

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  202: 	# removes the TOCTOU window that makes DNS-resolved checks illusory anyway.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  203: 	return 1 unless $host =~ /\A\d{1,3}(?:\.\d{1,3}){3}\z/;
  204: 
  205: 	my $packed = inet_aton($host) or return 1;
  206: 	my $n = unpack 'N', $packed;
  207: 
  208: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFF000000) == 0x0A000000;	# 10/8
  209: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFF00000) == 0xAC100000;	# 172.16/12
  210: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFFF0000) == 0xC0A80000;	# 192.168/16
  211: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFFF0000) == 0xA9FE0000;	# 169.254/16 (link-local / metadata)
  212: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFC00000) == 0x64400000;	# 100.64/10 (CGNAT)
  213: 	return 1;
  214: }

  216: # _safe_back_url($url) -> $url | undef
  217: #
  218: # Purpose: Sanitise a user-supplied back-link URL before it is passed to the
  219: #          template and rendered inside an href="" attribute.  Template Toolkit's

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  220: #          | html filter encodes <>&" but does NOT block the javascript: or

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  221: #          data: URI schemes, so a raw param('back') = "javascript:alert(1)"

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  222: #          would be rendered verbatim inside the href and execute on click.
  223: #
  224: # Rule:    Only absolute http(s) URLs and root-relative paths (starting with /)
  225: #          are permitted.  Anything else (javascript:, data:, vbscript:, empty
  226: #          strings, undef) returns undef so the template renders no href.
  227: #
  228: sub _safe_back_url {
  229: 	my ($url) = @_;
  230: 	return undef unless defined $url && length($url);
  231: 	return $url if $url =~ m{\A(?:/(?!/)|https?://)}i;
  232: 	return undef;
  233: }

  262: # _detect_platform($user_agent, $fallback) -> $platform_string
  263: #
  264: # Purpose: Map a raw User-Agent string to a VWF platform dimension
  265: #          ('mobile', 'tablet', or 'web') via CGI::Info.
  266: # Entry:   $user_agent -- the raw HTTP User-Agent header value (may be empty).

  268: # Exit:    Returns 'mobile', 'tablet', 'web', or $fallback.
  269: # Side Effects: temporarily sets $ENV{HTTP_USER_AGENT} with local().
  270: sub _detect_platform ($user_agent, $fallback) {
  271: 	my $platform = eval {
  272: 		local $ENV{HTTP_USER_AGENT} = $user_agent;
  273: 		my $info = CGI::Info->new();
  274: 		$info->is_mobile() ? 'mobile'
  275: 		: $info->is_tablet() ? 'tablet'
  276: 		:                      'web';
  277: 	};
  278: 	return $platform // $fallback;
  279: }

  324: # _scan_data_dir($self) -> \@table_list
  325: #
  326: # Purpose: Return an arrayref of { name, file } hashrefs for every supported

  328: #          by the join panel "available tables" dropdown.
  329: # Entry:   None.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  330: # Exit:    Returns [] when data_dir does not exist or contains no supported files.
  331: # Side Effects: Filesystem directory read.
  332: #
  333: # Optimisation: single-pass map replaces a two-pass grep+map chain, and calls
  334: # basename() only once per entry (the original chain called it twice: once in
  335: # grep to check the extension and again in map to extract the stem).
  336: # ->to_array avoids the extra flat-list expansion that ->each produced.
  337: sub _scan_data_dir :Protected ($self) {
  338: 	my $dir = $self->app->home->child($self->app->config->{data_dir} // 'data');
  339: 	return [] unless -d $dir;
  340: 	return $dir->list->map(sub {
  341: 		my $base = $_->basename;
  342: 		return unless $base =~ $EXT_RE;
  343: 		(my $name = $base) =~ s/\.[^.]+\z//;
  344: 		{ name => $name, file => $base, path => $_->to_string }
  345: 	})->to_array;
  346: }

  457: # _apply_filter_spec($records, $spec) -> \@filtered_records
  458: #
  459: # Purpose: Apply one "col:op:val" filter to an arrayref of record hashrefs and
  460: #          return a new (possibly shorter) arrayref.  The original is not mutated.
  461: #          Value may contain colons: "col:op:val:with:colons" -- split limit 3
  462: #          puts everything after the second colon into $val.
  463: #          Unknown operators fall through to 1 (all rows pass) so new operators
  464: #          added in future do not break existing callers.
  465: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $spec is a colon-delimited string.
  466: # Exit:    Returns filtered arrayref (same reference if spec is invalid/unparseable).
  467: #
  468: # Domain Constraints (operator names):
  469: #   Operator names are CASE-SENSITIVE.  Only the exact lowercase forms below are
  470: #   recognised; an uppercase or mixed-case name (e.g. "EQ", "Lt") falls through
  471: #   to the default `1` branch and all rows pass unfiltered.
  472: #   Valid operators:
  473: #     String (value comparison is case-insensitive via lc()):
  474: #       eq, ne, contains, starts
  475: #     Numeric (Perl <, <=, >, >= coercion):
  476: #       lt (strict), le (inclusive), gt (strict), ge (inclusive)
  477: #     Unary (val is ignored):
  478: #       empty (cell eq ''), notempty (cell ne '')
  479: #   Boundary rules for numeric operators:
  480: #     le/ge include the boundary value; lt/gt exclude it.
  481: #   contains/starts with empty val ('') match every record (index always 0).
  482: #
  483: # Reduction: split(/:/, $spec, 3) always produces at least one defined element,

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  484: # so "defined $col" is structurally guaranteed true and is removed.
  485: # "$_->{$col} // ''" replaces the equivalent ternary form.
  486: #
  487: # Optimisation: $lval = lc($val) is computed once before the grep.  Without this
  488: # precompute, every case-insensitive operator (eq, ne, contains, starts) would
  489: # call lc($val) O(N) times for N records -- a pure O(N) waste since $val is
  490: # constant within a single filter application.

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  491: sub _apply_filter_spec {

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  492: 	my ($records, $spec) = @_;

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  493: 	my ($col, $op, $val) = split /:/, $spec, 3;

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  494: 	return $records unless length($col // '') && defined $op && length $op;

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  495: 	$val //= '';

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  496: 	my $lval = lc $val;	# precompute once; avoids O(N) redundant lc() inside grep
  497: 	return [grep {
  498: 		my $cell = $_->{$col} // '';
  499: 		$op eq 'eq'       ? lc($cell) eq $lval                :
  500: 		$op eq 'ne'       ? lc($cell) ne $lval                :
  501: 		$op eq 'contains' ? index(lc($cell), $lval) != -1     :
  502: 		$op eq 'starts'   ? index(lc($cell), $lval) == 0      :
  503: 		$op eq 'lt'       ? $cell <  $val                     :
  504: 		$op eq 'le'       ? $cell <= $val                     :
  505: 		$op eq 'gt'       ? $cell >  $val                     :
  506: 		$op eq 'ge'       ? $cell >= $val                     :
  507: 		$op eq 'empty'    ? $cell eq ''                        :
  508: 		$op eq 'notempty' ? $cell ne ''                        :
  509: 		1
  510: 	} @$records];
  511: }

  513: # _apply_filters($self, $records) -> ($filtered_records, \@raw_specs, $json_string)
  514: #
  515: # Purpose: Read all "f=" query params, apply them in order via _apply_filter_spec,
  516: #          and return: the filtered arrayref, the raw spec strings (for building
  517: #          export URLs), and a script-safe JSON string for pre-populating the UI.
  518: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs.
  519: # Exit:    Three-element list; never croaks.  $json_string has '</' escaped to
  520: #          '<\/' so it is safe to embed directly in a <script> block.

  523: # apply filters, one to parse them for JSON.  By Modus Ponens, _apply_filter_spec
  524: # returns $records unchanged for a malformed spec (same guard condition), so
  525: # skipping the call for malformed specs is equivalent.  Merging into one loop
  526: # eliminates a full O(n) pass and the redundant split() on every spec.
  527: sub _apply_filters :Protected ($self, $records) {

  532: 		next unless length($col // '') && defined $op && length $op;
  533: 		push @parsed, { col => $col, op => $op, val => $val // '' };
  534: 		$records = _apply_filter_spec($records, $s);
  535: 	}
  536: 	my $json = encode_json(\@parsed);
  537: 	$json =~ s{</}{<\\/}g;
  538: 	return ($records, \@specs, $json);
  539: }

  558: # _filter_specs_to_criteria( \@specs, $source ) -> ( \%criteria, \@residual_specs )
  559: #
  560: # Purpose: Map the f= filter vocabulary onto Database::Abstraction / Database::Join
  561: #          criteria hashrefs so the backend can apply pushable filters before
  562: #          paginating.  Specs that cannot be safely pushed are returned as
  563: #          @residual_specs for subsequent in-memory application.
  564: #
  565: # Pushdown is only performed when $source->dbi_source() confirms a SQL (SQLite)
  566: # backend.  For CSV (slurp path), in-memory, XLSX, BerkeleyDB and URL sources
  567: # everything is placed in @residual_specs so the existing _apply_filter_spec
  568: # Perl path handles them.
  569: #
  570: # Pushable mapping (SQLite only):
  571: #   eq / ne         -- LIKE / NOT LIKE without wildcards.  SQLite LIKE is
  572: #                      case-insensitive for ASCII, matching the existing Perl
  573: #                      lc() semantics.  Values containing % or _ are pushed to
  574: #                      residual to avoid LIKE metacharacter conflicts.
  575: #   contains        -- LIKE '%val%'.  Residual when value contains % or _.
  576: #   starts          -- LIKE 'val%'.   Residual when value contains % or _.
  577: #   lt / le / gt / ge -- SQL numeric comparison.  Residual when the value is
  578: #                      not a plain number (avoids column-affinity surprises
  579: #                      with currency or comma-formatted values).
  580: #   empty / notempty -- Always residual.  Must match both SQL NULL and the
  581: #                      literal empty string; cannot be expressed as a single
  582: #                      D::A criterion without an -or grouping.
  583: #
  584: # Column collision: if a column already has a pushable criterion, the second
  585: # spec for that column is placed in @residual_specs.  This keeps the AND logic
  586: # simple and safe; callers with multiple conditions on one column (e.g. a range)
  587: # still get correct results via the in-memory fallback.

  671: # _numeric_cell($v) -> $number | undef
  672: #
  673: # Extract a numeric value from a raw cell, handling currency prefixes
  674: # (e.g. $1,234.56) and accounting-notation negatives ((1,234.56) -> -1234.56).
  675: # Returns undef for empty, undef, or non-numeric input.
  676: # Used by the Perl fold path of _grouped_totals so all four chart routes
  677: # share one extraction function.
  678: sub _numeric_cell {
  679: 	my $v = shift;
  680: 	return undef unless defined $v && length($v);
  681: 	my $is_acct_neg = ($v =~ /\A\s*\(/);
  682: 	(my $n = $v) =~ s/[^\d.\-]//g;

  684: 	return undef unless $n =~ /\A-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?\z/;
  685: 	return $n + 0;
  686: }

 2524: # _decode_cell($s) -> $character_string
 2525: #
 2526: # Purpose: Ensure $s is a Perl character string, not raw UTF-8 bytes.
 2527: #          URL-fetched data (via LWP::UserAgent::Cached + D::A HTML parsing)
 2528: #          arrives as byte strings without the UTF-8 flag set.  encode_json
 2529: #          then treats each byte as a Latin-1 code point and re-encodes it,
 2530: #          producing mojibake (e.g. an o-umlaut character displayed as two garbage bytes).  This helper
 2531: #          upgrades the flag in-place on a copy so the original hash key is
 2532: #          unchanged but the value passed to HTML::D3 is a proper character.
 2533: # Entry:   $s -- any defined scalar (undef returned as-is).
 2534: # Exit:    The same string value, but with the UTF-8 flag set if it was absent.
 2535: sub _decode_cell {
 2536: 	my $s = shift;
 2537: 	return $s unless defined $s;
 2538: 	utf8::decode($s) unless utf8::is_utf8($s);
 2539: 	$s;
 2540: }

 2542: # _serialize_csv($records, \@columns) -> $utf8_string
 2543: #
 2544: # Purpose: Build a complete RFC 4180 CSV document (header + data rows) from
 2545: #          $records.  Shared by _render_csv (HTTP download) and the csv branch
 2546: #          of export_write (filesystem write) to eliminate duplicated logic.
 2547: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $columns is an arrayref of names.
 2548: # Exit:    Returns a UTF-8 Perl string ending with CRLF.
 2549: #
 2550: # Optimisation: rows are collected in @lines and joined in one operation.
 2551: # The previous pattern ($out .= _csv_row(...) per row) triggers O(N) string
 2552: # reallocations; joining a pre-built array is a single O(total_bytes) allocation.
 2553: # For exports of thousands of rows the difference is measurable.
 2554: #
 2555: # XS note: for very large exports, replacing _csv_row with Text::CSV_XS

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)

 2558: sub _serialize_csv {
●2559 → 2561 → 2564 2559: 	my ($records, $columns) = @_;

 2561: 	for my $row (@$records) {
 2562: 		push @lines, _csv_row(map { $row->{$_} } @$columns);
 2563: 	}
 2564: 	return encode('UTF-8', join('', @lines));
 2565: }

 2656: =head2 view
 2657: 
 2658: C<GET /view/:table> -- Open and display the chosen table from C<data_dir>.
 2659: 
 2660: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2661: 
 2662: =head4 INPUT
 2663: 
 2664:   :table   string   Table name (alphanumeric + underscore).  Returns 404 on
 2665:                     other characters.
 2666:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 2667: 
 2668: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: :table
 2669: 
 2670: C<:table> is validated against C<\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z> before C<open_table>
 2671: is ever called.  The first character must be a letter (A-Z, a-z) or an
 2672: underscore; subsequent characters may also include digits (0-9).
 2673: 
 2674: =over 4
 2675: 
 2676: =item Valid partition
 2677: 
 2678: C<sales> (letters only), C<_temp> (underscore-start), C<report_2024>
 2679: (mixed).  A name that is valid but has no backing file returns 200 with
 2680: an error message -- NOT 404.
 2681: 
 2682: =item Invalid partition
 2683: 
 2684: C<1sales> (digit-start, 404), C<my.data> (dot, 404), C<my-data>
 2685: (hyphen, 404), non-ASCII characters (404).
 2686: 
 2687: =item Boundary values
 2688: 
 2689: C<a> (single letter, valid), C<_> (single underscore, valid), C<1>
 2690: (single digit, 404), C<a1> (letter then digit, valid), C<1a> (digit
 2691: then letter, 404).
 2692: 
 2693: =back

 2887: =head2 open_file
 2888: 
 2889: C<GET /open> -- Open a supported data file from any absolute filesystem path.
 2890: 
 2891: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2892: 
 2893: =head4 INPUT
 2894: 
 2895:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to the data file.  Returns 404 when missing,
 2896:                     not a regular file, or the extension is not in SUPPORTED_EXT.
 2897:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 2898: 
 2899: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: ?path=
 2900: 
 2901: =over 4
 2902: 
 2903: =item Extension filter (C<EXT_RE>)
 2904: 
 2905: The basename must match C<\.(?:csv|db|sql|xml|tsv|psv)\z> (case-insensitive).
 2906: The C<\z> anchor (absolute end-of-string) means a URL-encoded trailing
 2907: newline (e.g. C<file.csv%0A> decoded to C<file.csv\n>) does NOT pass --
 2908: the C<\n> falls after the C<\z> boundary and the extension check fails.
 2909: 
 2910: =item Valid partition
 2911: 
 2912: C</data/sales.csv> (lowercase extension), C</tmp/REPORT.CSV> (uppercase
 2913: extension, /i matches), any C<.db>, C<.sql>, C<.xml>, C<.tsv>, C<.psv> regular
 2914: file.
 2915: 
 2916: =item Invalid partition
 2917: 
 2918: Absent C<?path=> (404), non-existent file (404), directory instead of
 2919: file (404), unsupported extension such as C<.txt> (404), empty string
 2920: (404), path containing a C<%0A> (newline) suffix (404).
 2921: 
 2922: =item Double-extension filenames
 2923: 
 2924: A file like C<file.php.csv> passes C<EXT_RE> (last segment is C<.csv>)

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2925: but produces table stem C<file.php> which fails C<DataSource>'s
 2926: C<TABLE_NAME_RE>.  The C<open_table> call is inside C<eval>, so the
 2927: croak is caught and the action returns 200 with a friendly error, not
 2928: a 500.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2929: 
 2930: =back
 2931: 
 2932: =head4 OUTPUT

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
 2933: 
 2934: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with C<back_url> pointing to the
 2935: directory's C</browse> page, and C<file_path> set to the resolved absolute
 2936: path (used by the template to record the file in C<localStorage>).
 2937: On error re-renders C<home.html.tt> with C<error>, C<back_url>, C<back_label>.
 2938:

 3289: =head2 columns_api
 3290: 
 3291: C<GET /api/columns> -- Return column names for a table or file as JSON.
 3292: 
 3293: Used by the join UI to populate the right-key dropdown without a page reload.
 3294: 
 3295: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3296: 
 3297: =head4 INPUT
 3298: 
 3299:   ?table=  string   Table name from C<data_dir>.
 3300:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to a data file.
 3301:   ?spec=   string   Unified spec: C<"table:name"> or C<"path:/abs/path">.
 3302:                     Parsed only when C<table> and C<path> are both absent.
 3303: 
 3304: One of C<table>, C<path>, or C<spec> must be present; C<table> takes
 3305: precedence over C<path>, and both take precedence over C<spec>.
 3306: 
 3307: =head4 OUTPUT
 3308: 
 3309:   200 application/json   { "columns": ["col1", "col2", ...] }
 3310:   404 application/json   { "error": "not found" }
 3311:

 3383: 
 3384: C<GET /join> -- Perform one or more left joins and render the merged table.
 3385: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3386: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3387: 
 3388: =head4 INPUT
 3389: 
 3390:   ?l=    string   Required. Left table spec: "table:name" or "path:/abs/path".
 3391:                   Returns 404 if unresolvable.
 3392:   ?j=    string   (repeatable) Join step: "<right-spec>|<left-key>|<right-key>".
 3393:                   Invalid or non-existent steps are silently skipped.
 3394:   ?f=    string   (repeatable) Result filter: "col:op:val".
 3395: 
 3396: =head4 OUTPUT
 3397: 
 3398: Renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the merged/filtered result set.
 3399: 
 3400: =head3 MESSAGES
 3401: 
 3402:   error_table_open   -- Left table fetch threw (re-renders home with error).
 3403: 
 3404: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3405: 
 3406:   join_tables == lambda self .
 3407:     let left = open_spec(param('l')) in
 3408:     pre  left /= undef
 3409:     let joined  = fold(_left_join, left, param_list('j')) in

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
 3410:     let filtered = fold(_apply_filter_spec, joined, param_list('f')) in
 3411:     post render(dashboard, records: filtered)
 3412: 
 3413: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 3414: 
 3415:   1. Parse and open left table spec; 404 on failure.
 3416:   2. For each j= param (in order):
 3417:        a. Parse right-spec, left-key, right-key from "|"-split.
 3418:        b. Verify left-key exists in current column list.
 3419:        c. Open right table; skip step on any error.
 3420:        d. Verify right-key exists in right column list.
 3421:        e. Call _left_join; update records and columns.
 3422:   3. Apply all f= filters via _apply_filter_spec.
 3423:   4. Build stable table key for localStorage ("join:left:right1:...").
 3424:   5. Render dashboard.
 3425: 
 3426: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3427: 
 3428:   GET /join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 3429:     -> merged left join of sales and products on product/name columns
 3430: 
 3431: =cut

 3547: =head2 combine_tables
 3548: 
 3549: C<GET /combine> -- Stack rows from two or more tables vertically into a single

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
 3550: unified view.
 3551: 
 3552: Unlike C</join> (which appends columns from a matching row in a second table),
 3553: C</combine> appends the I<rows> from a second table beneath the rows of the

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3554: first.  All columns from all sources appear as headers; where a row has no
 3555: value for a particular column (because that column did not exist in its source
 3556: file) the cell is left blank.
 3557: 
 3558: This is equivalent to a SQL C<UNION ALL> across heterogeneous schemas.
 3559: 
 3560: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3561: 
 3562: =head4 INPUT
 3563: 
 3564:   ?l=    string   (required) Left table spec: "table:name" or "path:/abs/path".

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3565:                   Returns 404 if unresolvable.
 3566:   ?c=    string   (repeatable) Additional table spec to stack beneath the
 3567:                   current result.  Invalid or non-existent specs are silently
 3568:                   skipped.
 3569:   ?f=    string   (repeatable) Result filter: "col:op:val".
 3570: 
 3571: =head4 OUTPUT
 3572: 
 3573: Renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the combined result set.
 3574: 
 3575: =head3 MESSAGES
 3576: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 3577:   error_table_open   -- Left table fetch threw (re-renders home with error).
 3578: 
 3579: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3580: 
 3581:   combine_tables == lambda self .
 3582:     let left = open_spec(param('l')) in
 3583:     pre  left /= undef
 3584:     let sources  = [left] ++ map(open_spec, param_list('c')) in
 3585:     let combined = _combine_tables(sources) in
 3586:     let filtered = fold(_apply_filter_spec, combined, param_list('f')) in
 3587:     post render(dashboard, records: filtered)
 3588: 
 3589: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3590: 
 3591:   GET /combine?l=table:cats&c=table:dogs
 3592:     -> unified view: all cat and dog rows; Species/Name/Color/Breed/Eye Color

 3793:   filename=  string   Output filename including extension.  Extension determines
 3794:                       format: C<.csv> -> RFC 4180 CSV; C<.sql> -> SQLite;
 3795:                       C<.json> -> JSON array of row objects.
 3796: 
 3797: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: filename=
 3798: 
 3799: The extension check uses C</\.csv\z/i> (CSV), C</\.sql\z/i> (SQLite), or
 3800: C</\.json\z/i> (JSON): the C</i> flag makes matching case-insensitive, so
 3801: C<.CSV>, C<.SQL>, and C<.JSON> are accepted alongside lowercase forms.
 3802: Everything else returns 415.
 3803: 
 3804: Path separator characters (C</> and C<\>) in C<filename> are stripped
 3805: first via C<m{([^/\\]+)\z}> -- only the basename is kept, preventing
 3806: directory traversal.

 3809:

 3811: 
 3812: C<report.csv>, C<report.sql>, C<report.json>, C<REPORT.CSV>, C<report.SQL>.
 3813: A single-character stem (C<a.csv>) is also valid.

 4056: C<POST /upload> -- Accept a drag-and-dropped data file and return a redirect URL.
 4057: 
 4058: The file is saved under its original filename in a managed subdirectory of
 4059: C<< <app_home>/.uploads/ >>.  The subdirectory name is randomised so that
 4060: concurrent uploads of files with the same name do not collide.
 4061: 
 4062: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4063: 
 4064: =head4 INPUT
 4065:

 4067: 
 4068:   file   upload   Supported extensions: csv, db, sql, xml, tsv, psv.
 4069: 
 4070: =head4 OUTPUT
 4071: 
 4072:   200 application/json   { "url": "/open?path=/abs/path/file.csv", "path": "/abs/path/file.csv" }
 4073:   400 application/json   { "error": "No file received" }

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)

 4077: 
 4078: =head3 MESSAGES
 4079: 
 4080:   error_upload_none       -- no file was received in the multipart upload.
 4081:   error_upload_ext        -- the file's extension is not in the supported list.
 4082:   error_upload_too_large  -- the upload body exceeded MAX_UPLOAD_MIB (50 MiB).
 4083:   error_upload_save       -- move_to failed or the destination file was not
 4084:                              created (I/O error, full disk, permission error).
 4085: 
 4086: =head3 IMPLEMENTATION NOTES
 4087: 
 4088: The C<move_to> call is wrapped with the C<eval { ...; 1 }> idiom (not
 4089: C<eval {}> + C<if ($@)>).  Mojolicious dispatch and DESTROY-phase evals can
 4090: leave C<$@> set before the action runs; checking C<if ($@)> after a new eval
 4091: may fire falsely on that stale value.  C<eval { move_to(); 1 }> returns 1 on
 4092: success and C<undef> on exception, so the result is independent of any prior
 4093: C<$@> value.
 4094: 
 4095: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4096: 
 4097:   upload_file == lambda self .

 4101:          /\ upload.size <= MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES
 4102:     let dest = home/.uploads/<random>/<filename> in
 4103:     let ok = eval { upload.move_to(dest); 1 } in
 4104:     pre  ok /\ -f dest
 4105:     post render_json({ url: '/open?path=' ++ url_escape(dest), path: dest })
 4106: 
 4107: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4108: 
 4109:   POST /upload  (multipart: file=@sales.csv)
 4110:     -> { "url": "/open?path=%2F...%2Fsales.csv", "path": "/.../.uploads/abc123/sales.csv" }

 4112: =cut

 4527: =head2 heatmap_view
 4528: 
 4529: C<GET /heatmap> -- Render a D3.js grid heatmap for two categorical axes.
 4530: 
 4531: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 4532: 
 4533:   x=<col>      X-axis (horizontal) categorical column (required)
 4534:   y=<col>      Y-axis (vertical) categorical column (required)
 4535:   val=<col>    Cell value column (optional; when absent, cells show row count)
 4536:   scheme=<name> D3 sequential colour scheme name (default: YlOrRd)
 4537:   show_val=1   Overlay the numeric value inside each cell
 4538:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 4539: 
 4540: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4541: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 4542: =head4 INPUT
 4543: 
 4544:   l       spec     Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 4545:   x       string   Required.  Name of the X-axis categorical column.
 4546:   y       string   Required.  Name of the Y-axis categorical column.
 4547:   val     string   Optional.  Name of the value column; omit for count mode.
 4548:   scheme  string   Optional.  D3 sequential colour scheme name.
 4549:   show_val flag    Optional.  Set to 1 to render values inside cells.
 4550:   back    url      Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 4551: 
 4552: =head4 OUTPUT
 4553: 
 4554:   200 text/html           Rendered heatmap page.
 4555:   200 text/plain          "No plottable data: ..." when grid is empty.
 4556:   400 text/plain          Missing x= or y= parameter.
 4557:   400 text/plain          Column not found: <col>.
 4558:   404 text/plain          "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 4559: 
 4560: =head3 MESSAGES
 4561: 
 4562:   error_heatmap_no_data -- no (x,y) pair has a non-empty value.
 4563: 
 4564: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4565: 
 4566:   heatmap_view == lambda self .
 4567:     pre length(x_col) > 0 /\ length(y_col) > 0
 4568:         /\ x_col in columns /\ y_col in columns
 4569:     let triples = { (row[x_col], row[y_col], row[val_col]) | row in records,
 4570:                     all three fields non-empty } in
 4571:     post render(heatmap, triples)
 4572: 
 4573: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4574: 
 4575:   GET /heatmap?l=table:sales&x=region&y=product&val=amount
 4576:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 heatmap SVG
 4577: 
 4578: =cut
 4579:

 4707: 
 4708: =head3 API SPECIFICATION

 4715:                    omit entirely to count rows per category.

 4867: 
 4868: =head2 folder_view
 4869: 
 4870: C<GET /folder> -- Browse a data table grouped by the letter-prefix hierarchy
 4871: of an order-number column (e.g. A10, CT4, B3).
 4872: 
 4873: The top level displays one folder icon per initial letter group (A, B, C ...).
 4874: Clicking a folder with sub-prefixes (e.g. C contains CA and CB items) shows
 4875: the sub-folders; clicking a leaf prefix shows the actual data rows sorted
 4876: numerically by the embedded digit sequence.
 4877: 
 4878: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 4879: 
 4880:   col=<col>      Column whose values contain order codes (optional; auto-
 4881:                  detected when absent by scanning the first 10 rows for
 4882:                  values matching C<[A-Z]{1,4}\d+[A-Z]?>)
 4883:   prefix=<str>   Current drill-down prefix, e.g. "CA" (optional; top-level
 4884:                  when absent)
 4885:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional;
 4886:                  derived from C<l=> when absent)
 4887: 
 4888: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4889: 
 4890: =head4 INPUT
 4891: 
 4892:   l       spec     Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 4893:   col     string   Optional.  Order-code column name.
 4894:   prefix  string   Optional.  Current letter prefix; max length MAX_PREFIX_LEN.
 4895:   back    url      Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 4896: 
 4897: =head4 OUTPUT
 4898: 
 4899:   200 text/html    Rendered folder page with folder grid and/or item table.
 4900:   400 text/plain   "Missing l= parameter" when l= is absent.
 4901:   400 text/plain   "Prefix too long" when prefix exceeds MAX_PREFIX_LEN (20).
 4902:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when the data source is unavailable.
 4903: 
 4904: =head3 MESSAGES
 4905: 
 4906: None -- all failure paths render plain text with an HTTP status code.
 4907: 
 4908: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4909: 
 4910:   folder_view == lambda self .
 4911:     pre length(left_spec) > 0 /\ length(prefix) <= MAX_PREFIX_LEN
 4912:     let records = pipeline(left_spec, joins, filters)
 4913:         prefix_map = { p -> [row] | row in records, row[col] =~ /[A-Z]+/ -> p } in
 4914:     IF prefix = "" THEN
 4915:       post render(folder, folders = first_letters(prefix_map))
 4916:     ELSE
 4917:       post render(folder, folders = sub_prefixes(prefix, prefix_map),
 4918:                           items   = sort_numeric(prefix_map[prefix]))
 4919: 
 4920: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4921: 
 4922:   GET /folder?l=table:orders&col=order_no
 4923:     -> 200 HTML folder grid showing A (12 items), B (7 items), C (4 items)
 4924: 
 4925:   GET /folder?l=table:orders&col=order_no&prefix=A
 4926:     -> 200 HTML folder grid for sub-prefixes and items directly under "A"
 4927: 
 4928: =cut
 4929: 
 4930: sub folder_view ($self) {

 4973: 		next unless defined $p;

 4975: 	}

●4980 → 5012 → 5059 4980: 	my $sub_prefixes = sub {
 4981: 		my ($par) = @_;
 4982: 		my $len = length($par);
 4983: 		my %seen;
 4984: 		for my $p (keys %prefix_to_rows) {
 4985: 			next if length($p) <= $len;
 4986: 			next if substr($p, 0, $len) ne $par;
 4987: 			$seen{ substr($p, 0, $len + 1) } = 1;
 4988: 		}
 4989: 		return sort keys %seen;
 4990: 	};

 4993: 	my $deep_count = sub {
 4994: 		my ($par) = @_;

 4996: 		for my $p (keys %prefix_to_rows) {
 4997: 			$n += scalar @{ $prefix_to_rows{$p} }
 4998: 				if substr($p, 0, length($par)) eq $par;
 4999: 		}

 5025: 	} else {

 5027: 		# e.g. prefix="CD" -> [ {name="C", href=...}, {name="CD", href=undef} ]

 5048: 		@items = @{ $prefix_to_rows{$prefix} // [] };

 5051: 		@items = sort {

 5053: 			my ($bn) = ($b->{$key_col} // '') =~ /(\d+)/;
 5054: 			($an // 0) <=> ($bn // 0)
 5055: 				|| ($a->{$key_col} // '') cmp ($b->{$key_col} // '');
 5056: 		} @items;
 5057: 	}
 5058: 
 5059: 	my $title = length($prefix) ? "Folder: $prefix" : 'Folder view';
 5060: 
 5061: 	$self->render(
 5062: 		handler    => 'tt',
 5063: 		template   => "$platform/$language/folder",
 5064: 		format     => 'html',
 5065: 		title      => $title,
 5066: 		folders    => \@folders,

 5099:   back_url   string   URL to navigate back one level

 5643: 
 5644: =head2 stats_view
 5645: 
 5646: C<GET /stats> -- Column statistics panel.
 5647: 
 5648: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join> and displays
 5649: per-column statistics: min, max, mean, median, null_count, distinct_count,
 5650: row_count.
 5651: 
 5652: Statistics are computed through C<_grouped_totals> so they agree with the pivot
 5653: view.  Median is computed in Perl by sorting the column values.
 5654: 
 5655: =cut
 5656: 
 5657: sub stats_view ($self) {
●5658 → 5671 → 5673 5658: 	my $left_spec = $self->param('l') // '';

 5680: 	my @stats;
 5681: 	for my $col (@{$columns}) {
 5682: 		# null_count = rows where cell is undef or '' (phase 2 definition).
 5683: 		my @nonnull    = grep { defined $_->{$col} && length($_->{$col}) } @$records;
 5684: 		my $null_count = $row_count - scalar @nonnull;
 5685: 
 5686: 		# distinct_count via _grouped_totals (count groups by the column).
 5687: 		my $dist_rows      = $self->_grouped_totals($pipeline, {
 5688: 			by => [$col],

 5690: 			as => 'cnt',
 5691: 		});
 5692: 		my $distinct_count = scalar @$dist_rows;

 5695: 		my @nums = grep { defined $_ } map { _numeric_cell($_->{$col}) } @nonnull;
 5696: 		my ($min_val, $max_val, $avg_val, $median_val);
 5697: 		if (@nums) {
 5698: 			my @sorted = sort { $a <=> $b } @nums;
 5699: 			$min_val = $sorted[0];
 5700: 			$max_val = $sorted[-1];
 5701: 			my $sum  = 0;
 5702: 			$sum += $_ for @nums;
 5703: 			$avg_val = $sum / @nums;
 5704: 			my $mid  = int(@sorted / 2);
 5705: 			$median_val = @sorted % 2
 5706: 				? $sorted[$mid]
 5707: 				: ($sorted[$mid - 1] + $sorted[$mid]) / 2;
 5708: 		}
 5709: 
 5710: 		push @stats, {
 5711: 			col            => $col,
 5712: 			row_count      => $row_count,
 5713: 			null_count     => $null_count,
 5714: 			distinct_count => $distinct_count,
 5715: 			(defined $min_val ? (
 5716: 				min    => $min_val,
 5717: 				max    => $max_val,
 5718: 				avg    => sprintf('%.4g', $avg_val),
 5719: 				median => sprintf('%.4g', $median_val),
 5720: 			) : ()),
 5721: 		};
 5722: 	}
 5723: 
 5724: 	(my $left_label = $left_spec) =~ s/\A(?:table:|path:|url:)//;
 5725: 
 5726: 	my ($platform, $language) = $self->_resolve_template;
 5727: 	$self->render(
 5728: 		handler    => 'tt',
 5729: 		template   => "$platform/$language/stats",
 5730: 		format     => 'html',
 5731: 		title          => 'Column statistics',
 5732: 		stats          => \@stats,
 5733: 		row_count      => $row_count,
 5734: 		left_spec      => $left_spec,
 5735: 		left_label     => $left_label,
 5736: 		drill_back_url => $back,
 5737: 		drill_sep      => (CORE::index($back, '?') >= 0 ? '&' : '?'),
 5738: 		stats_url      => $self->req->url->path_query,
 5739: 		back_url       => ($back2 // $back),
 5740: 		back_label     => ($back2 ? ($back2_lbl || 'Back') : ($self->param('back_label') // 'Back to table')),
 5741: 		($back2 ? (back2_url => $back, back2_label => ($self->param('back_label') // 'Back to table')) : ()),
 5742: 	);
 5743: }

 6373: 	$bins = 200 if $bins > 200;
 6374: 
 6375: 	# Server-side binning: compute equal-width bins over the data range.
 6376: 	# Values equal to $max land in the highest bin (no overflow bar).
 6377: 	# Uses the same bin-boundary logic as Calc.pm bin() to stay consistent with
 6378: 	# ?c=band:bin(col,width) computed columns on the same data.
 6379: 	use POSIX qw(floor);
 6380: 	my $min_v = min(@values);
 6381: 	my $max_v = max(@values);
 6382: 	my @bin_data;
 6383: 	if ($min_v == $max_v) {
 6384: 		# All values identical: one bin.
 6385: 		push @bin_data, { label => "$min_v", count => scalar @values, lo => $min_v, hi => $min_v };
 6386: 	} else {
 6387: 		my $raw_w = ($max_v - $min_v) / $bins;
 6388: 		# Round up to a "nice" width using 10 significant digits (same guard as Calc bin()).
 6389: 		my $width = sprintf('%.10g', $raw_w) + 0;
 6390: 		$width = $raw_w if $width <= 0;  # safety: never produce zero width
 6391: 		my $fmt = ($width == int($width)) ? '%d' : '%g';
 6392: 
 6393: 		# Build bin boundaries.
 6394: 		my @bin_counts;
 6395: 		my @bin_los;
 6396: 		for my $i (0 .. $bins - 1) {
 6397: 			push @bin_los, $min_v + $i * $width;
 6398: 			push @bin_counts, 0;
 6399: 		}
 6400: 
 6401: 		# Assign each value to a bin.
 6402: 		for my $v (@values) {
 6403: 			my $idx = floor(sprintf('%.10g', ($v - $min_v) / $width) + 0);
 6404: 			$idx = $bins - 1 if $idx >= $bins;  # max value -> last bin
 6405: 			$idx = 0         if $idx < 0;
 6406: 			$bin_counts[$idx]++;
 6407: 		}
 6408: 
 6409: 		for my $i (0 .. $bins - 1) {
 6410: 			next unless $bin_counts[$i];
 6411: 			my $lo  = $bin_los[$i];
 6412: 			my $hi  = $lo + $width;
 6413: 			my $lbl = sprintf("[${fmt},${fmt})", $lo, $hi);
 6414: 			push @bin_data, { label => $lbl, count => $bin_counts[$i], lo => $lo, hi => $hi };
 6415: 		}
 6416: 	}
 6417: 
 6418: 	my ($source_url, $source_accessed) = $self->_url_attribution;
 6419: 
 6420: 	require HTML::D3;
 6421: 	my $title   = "Distribution of $col";
 6422: 	my $snippet = HTML::D3->new(title => $title, width => 800, height => 480, responsive => 1)
 6423: 		->render_histogram_snippet(\@bin_data, {
 6424: 			binned  => 1,
 6425: 			x_label => $col,
 6426: 		});
 6427: 
 6428: 	my $chart_html = $snippet->{html};
 6429: 	utf8::decode($chart_html) unless utf8::is_utf8($chart_html);
 6430: 
 6431: 	my %chart_spec = (
 6432: 		kind    => 'histogram',
 6433: 		title   => $title,
 6434: 		encode  => { value => $col },
 6435: 		options => { bins => $bins },
 6436: 	);
 6437: 	my ($platform, $language) = $self->_resolve_template;
 6438: 	$self->render(
 6439: 		handler          => 'tt',
 6440: 		template         => "$platform/$language/histogram",
 6441: 		format           => 'html',
 6442: 		title            => $title,
 6443: 		chart_html       => $chart_html,
 6444: 		chart            => \%chart_spec,
 6445: 		value_count      => scalar @values,
 6446: 		bins             => $bins,
 6447: 		back_url         => ($back2 // $back),
 6448: 		back_label       => ($back2 ? ($back2_lbl || 'Back') : 'Back to table'),
 6449: 		($back2 ? (back2_url => $back, back2_label => 'Back to table') : ()),
 6450: 		source_url       => $source_url,
 6451: 		source_accessed  => $source_accessed,
 6452: 	);
 6453: }
 6454: 
 6455: 1;
 6456: 
 6457: __END__
 6458: 
 6459: =head1 NAME
 6460: 
 6461: Database::BI::Controller::Dashboard - Home picker, filesystem browser, table
 6462: viewer, left-join engine, result filter, export, and file upload
 6463: 
 6464: =head1 SYNOPSIS
 6465: 
 6466: All routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.  You do not
 6467: call its methods directly -- Mojolicious dispatches HTTP requests to them
 6468: automatically.  The examples below show browser URLs and their curl
 6469: equivalents.
 6470: 
 6471: B<Open the home page and see all tables in the data directory:>
 6472: 
 6473:   # Browser
 6474:   http://localhost:3000/
 6475: 
 6476:   # curl
 6477:   curl http://localhost:3000/
 6478: 
 6479: B<View a single table (file: data/sales.csv):>
 6480: 
 6481:   # Browser
 6482:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 6483: 
 6484:   # curl
 6485:   curl http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 6486: 
 6487: B<Filter rows -- show only rows where region equals "North":>
 6488: 
 6489:   # Browser (add ?f=col:op:val to any view URL)
 6490:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North
 6491: 
 6492:   # Multiple filters -- "North" AND amount greater than 100:
 6493:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North&f=amount:gt:100
 6494: 
 6495:   # curl
 6496:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North'
 6497: 
 6498: B<Join two tables -- left-join sales with products on the product column:>
 6499: 
 6500:   # Browser
 6501:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 6502: 
 6503:   # The j= parameter is: right-table-spec | left-key | right-key
 6504:   # You can chain multiple joins:
 6505:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&j=table:regions|region|id
 6506: 
 6507: B<Open a file anywhere on the filesystem (not just in data/):>
 6508: 
 6509:   http://localhost:3000/open?path=/home/user/reports/q3.csv
 6510: 
 6511: B<Download the current view as a CSV file:>
 6512: 
 6513:   # Uses the same l=, j=, f= parameters as /join
 6514:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=csv
 6515: 
 6516:   # Download as a SQLite database file instead:
 6517:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=sqlite
 6518: 
 6519:   # Download a filtered + joined result:
 6520:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&f=region:eq:North&format=csv
 6521: 
 6522: B<Save the current view to a file on the server (instead of downloading):>
 6523: 
 6524:   # POST with form fields; format is inferred from the filename extension
 6525:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 6526:        -F l=table:sales \
 6527:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 6528:        -F filename=report.csv
 6529: 
 6530:   # Save as SQLite:
 6531:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 6532:        -F l=table:sales \
 6533:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 6534:        -F filename=report.sql
 6535: 
 6536: B<Get the column list for a table (used by the join UI):>
 6537: 
 6538:   curl http://localhost:3000/api/columns?table=sales
 6539:   # Returns: {"columns":["product","region","amount","date"]}
 6540: 
 6541: B<Check when a file was last modified (used by the tooltip on the home page):>
 6542: 
 6543:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/api/stat?path=/data/sales.csv'
 6544:   # Returns: {"exists":true,"path":"/data/sales.csv","mtime":1700000000,"size":1234}
 6545: 
 6546: B<Browse the filesystem to find a data file:>
 6547: 
 6548:   http://localhost:3000/browse
 6549:   http://localhost:3000/browse?path=/home/user/data
 6550: 
 6551: B<Upload a data file by dropping it onto the page (multipart form POST):>
 6552: 
 6553:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/upload \
 6554:        -F file=@/home/user/data/sales.csv
 6555:   # Returns: {"url":"/open?path=/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv","path":"/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv"}
 6556: 
 6557: =head2 graph_view
 6558: 
 6559: C<GET /graph> -- Render a D3.js v7 line chart from the current data pipeline.
 6560: 
 6561: Accepts the same pipeline parameters as L</join_tables> (C<l=>, C<j=>,
 6562: C<c=>, C<f=>, C<d=>) plus:
 6563: 
 6564: =over 4
 6565: 
 6566: =item C<x=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 6567: 
 6568: Column name to use as the X axis (required).  Any data type is accepted;
 6569: values are displayed as categorical labels.
 6570: 
 6571: =item C<y=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 6572: 
 6573: Column name to use as the Y axis (required).  Non-numeric characters
 6574: (currency symbols, commas, etc.) are stripped before comparison; rows
 6575: where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are silently skipped.
 6576: 
 6577: =item C<back=E<lt>urlE<gt>>
 6578: 
 6579: URL for the "Back to table" link rendered in the graph toolbar.  Defaults
 6580: to C</> when absent.  The dashboard JS sets this to the current page URL
 6581: before navigating.
 6582: 
 6583: =back
 6584: 
 6585: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6586: 
 6587: =head4 INPUT
 6588: 
 6589:   l=<spec>     string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 6590:   x=<col>      string   X-axis column name (required).
 6591:   y=<col>      string   Y-axis column name (required; must be numeric).
 6592:   back=<url>   string   Back-link URL (optional; default "/").
 6593:   j=, c=, f=, d=        Pipeline params -- see L</join_tables>.
 6594: 
 6595: =head4 OUTPUT
 6596: 
 6597:   200 text/html   TT-rendered graph page.  The HTML::D3 snippet embeds a
 6598:                   C<const data = [...]> JSON array where each element has:
 6599:                     { label: <x-value>, value: <y-value>,
 6600:                       extra: { <col>: <val>, ... } }
 6601:                   C<extra> contains every column not used as X or Y, so
 6602:                   the hover tooltip shows the complete row for each point.
 6603:                   The chart supports brush-to-zoom; a "Reset zoom" button
 6604:                   appears after a brush selection.  The toolbar shows the
 6605:                   count of plotted data points.
 6606: 
 6607:   400 text/plain  C<x=> or C<y=> absent, or named column not found.
 6608:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 6609: 
 6610: =head3 MESSAGES
 6611: 
 6612:   Missing x or y column parameter          C<x=> or C<y=> query param absent.
 6613:   Column not found: <name>                  Named column absent from result set.
 6614:   Could not open data source                Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 6615:   No plottable data: no rows have valid ... All rows lack a numeric Y value.
 6616: 
 6617: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6618: 
 6619:   GET /graph?l=table:sales&x=product&y=amount
 6620: 
 6621: Renders a line chart of C<amount> (Y) against C<product> (X).  Mousing over
 6622: a dot shows a tooltip with the product name, the amount, and every other
 6623: column in that row (e.g. C<region>, C<date>).
 6624: 
 6625: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6626: 
 6627:   graph_view : Controller Ã— Params â†’ HTML | Error
 6628: 
 6629:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, c, f, d)
 6630:   let pairs    = [ [row[x], num(row[y]), row \ {x,y}]
 6631:                    | row âˆˆ pipeline.records,  num(row[y]) â‰  âŠ¥ ]
 6632: 
 6633:   pre  x âˆˆ params âˆ§ y âˆˆ params           else 400
 6634:   pre  x âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 6635:   pre  y âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 6636:   pre  pairs â‰  []                          else 200 "No plottable data"
 6637:   post HTML::D3->render_line_chart_snippet(pairs) embedded in TT layout
 6638: 
 6639: =cut
 6640: 
 6641: =head2 pie_view
 6642: 
 6643: C<GET /pie> -- Render a D3.js v7 animated pie chart from the current data
 6644: pipeline.
 6645: 
 6646: Each row's category value (C<cat=>) is used as a slice label; the numeric
 6647: value column (C<val=>) is summed per category.  Accounting-notation
 6648: negatives such as C<(1,234.56)> are handled automatically.  Slices are
 6649: sorted by total descending; when there are more than 12 distinct categories
 6650: the smallest ones are collapsed into a single "Other" slice.
 6651: 
 6652: Clicking any slice or legend entry navigates back to the table view with
 6653: an C<f=cat:eq:value> filter applied automatically, so the user can drill
 6654: into the data behind a slice without leaving the application.
 6655: 
 6656: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6657: 
 6658: =head4 INPUT
 6659: 
 6660:   l=<spec>    string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 6661:   cat=<col>   string   Category column name (required; used as slice labels).
 6662:   val=<col>   string   Numeric value column name (required; values are summed).
 6663:   donut=1     flag     Render as a donut chart with a hole in the centre (optional).
 6664:   back=<url>  string   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional; default "/").
 6665:   f=<spec>    string   Result filter (repeatable; same syntax as /join).
 6666: 
 6667: =head4 OUTPUT
 6668: 
 6669:   200 text/html   TT-rendered pie chart page containing:
 6670:                   - Two-level breadcrumb (Home > Back to table).
 6671:                   - Export buttons for SVG and PNG (legend is composited in).
 6672:                   - An animated D3.js pie or donut chart with a colour-coded
 6673:                     legend.  Each slice and legend entry is clickable and
 6674:                     navigates to the filtered table view.
 6675:                   - Slice count (e.g. "7 slices").
 6676: 
 6677:   200 text/plain  "No plottable data" when every row has a non-numeric or
 6678:                   missing value column (HTTP 200, not 4xx, so the page can
 6679:                   render a friendly message).
 6680: 
 6681:   400 text/plain  C<cat=> or C<val=> absent, or named column not found.
 6682:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 6683: 
 6684: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS
 6685: 
 6686:   cat=    Any column name that exists in the result set.  Cell values are
 6687:           used verbatim as slice labels.  Empty-string cells are skipped.
 6688: 
 6689:   val=    Must be a column whose cells contain numbers (after stripping
 6690:           currency symbols, commas, and handling accounting-notation negatives).
 6691:           Rows where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are skipped.
 6692:           Negative totals are included in the chart (slice values may be negative
 6693:           if the source data contains credits or reversals).
 6694: 
 6695:   donut=  Any truthy value (e.g. "1") enables the donut hole.  Absent or
 6696:           "0" renders a solid pie.
 6697: 
 6698: =head3 MESSAGES
 6699: 
 6700:   Missing cat or val column parameter    C<cat=> or C<val=> query param absent.
 6701:   Column not found: <name>               Named column absent from the result set.
 6702:   Could not open data source             Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 6703:   No plottable data: ...                 All rows lack a valid numeric value.
 6704: 
 6705: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6706: 
 6707:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&back=/view/sales
 6708: 
 6709: Renders a pie chart of total C<amount> per C<region>.  Clicking the
 6710: "North" slice navigates to C</view/sales?f=region:eq:North>.
 6711: 
 6712: With a donut hole:
 6713: 
 6714:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&donut=1
 6715: 
 6716: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6717: 
 6718:   pie_view : Controller x Params -> HTML | Error
 6719: 
 6720:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, f, d)
 6721:   let totals   = { cat => sum { val(row) | row in pipeline.records,
 6722:                                            cat(row) != '', num(val(row)) != bot }
 6723:                  | cat in range(cat_col) }
 6724: 
 6725:   pre  cat in params /\ val in params     else 400
 6726:   pre  cat in pipeline.cols               else 400
 6727:   pre  val in pipeline.cols               else 400
 6728:   pre  totals != {}                        else 200 "No plottable data"
 6729:   post HTML::D3->render_pie_chart_snippet(slices) embedded in TT layout
 6730:        where slices = sort_desc { |s.value| | s in totals }
 6731: 
 6732: =cut
 6733: 
 6734: =head2 clear_uploads
 6735: 
 6736: C<POST /uploads/clear> -- Delete every file from the C<.uploads/> staging
 6737: directory and return the total disk space recovered.
 6738: 
 6739: The C<.uploads/> directory accumulates files from drag-and-drop uploads.
 6740: It grows indefinitely; this action lets users reclaim that space without
 6741: needing shell access.
 6742: 
 6743: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6744: 
 6745: =head4 INPUT
 6746: 
 6747: No parameters.  The C<.uploads/> path is always the one under C<app->home>.
 6748: 
 6749: =head4 OUTPUT
 6750: 
 6751:   200 application/json   { "freed": <bytes>, "count": <n> }
 6752: 
 6753: C<freed> is the total number of bytes removed.  C<count> is the number of
 6754: files deleted.  Both are 0 when the directory is absent or already empty.
 6755: 
 6756: =cut
 6757: 
 6758: =head2 scatter_view
 6759: 
 6760: C<GET /scatter> -- Render a D3.js scatter plot or bubble chart.
 6761: 
 6762: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 6763: 
 6764:   x=<col>        X-axis column (required; numeric)
 6765:   y=<col>        Y-axis column (required; numeric)
 6766:   series=<col>   Colour-by category column (optional)
 6767:   size=<col>     Bubble-radius numeric column (optional)
 6768:   scheme=<name>  D3 colour scheme (default tableau10; only used with series=)
 6769:   min_r=N        Minimum bubble radius in px (default 3; range 1-40)
 6770:   max_r=N        Maximum bubble radius in px (default 15; range 1-40)
 6771:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 6772: 
 6773: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6774: 
 6775: =head4 INPUT
 6776: 
 6777:   l        spec    Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 6778:   x        string  Required.  Numeric X-axis column name.
 6779:   y        string  Required.  Numeric Y-axis column name.
 6780:   series   string  Optional.  Categorical colour-dimension column.
 6781:   size     string  Optional.  Numeric bubble-radius column.
 6782:   scheme   string  Optional.  One of: tableau10 category10 set2 set3 paired.
 6783:   min_r    int     Optional.  Minimum radius 1-40 (default 3).
 6784:   max_r    int     Optional.  Maximum radius 1-40 (default 15).
 6785:   back     url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 6786: 
 6787: =head4 OUTPUT
 6788: 
 6789:   200 text/html    Rendered scatter chart page.
 6790:   400 text/plain   Missing x= or y= parameter.
 6791:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 6792:   400 text/plain   Unknown colour scheme: <scheme>  (only when series= is set).
 6793:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 6794: 
 6795: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6796: 
 6797:   scatter_view == lambda self .
 6798:     pre  length(x_col) > 0 /\ length(y_col) > 0
 6799:          /\ x_col in columns /\ y_col in columns
 6800:          /\ (series_col != '' => series_col in columns)
 6801:          /\ (size_col   != '' => size_col   in columns)
 6802:          /\ (series_col != '' => scheme in SCATTER_SCHEMES)
 6803:          /\ 1 <= min_r <= 40 /\ 1 <= max_r <= 40
 6804:     post render(scatter, pairs)
 6805: 
 6806: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6807: 
 6808:   GET /scatter?l=table:sales&x=quantity&y=amount&series=region
 6809:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 scatter chart SVG
 6810: 
 6811: =cut
 6812: 
 6813: =head2 stacked_view
 6814: 
 6815: C<GET /stacked> -- Render a D3.js stacked bar chart.
 6816: 
 6817: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 6818: 
 6819:   cat=<col>     X-axis category column (required)
 6820:   series=<col>  Stack-segment column (required; seg= is a backwards-compat alias)
 6821:   val=<col>     Numeric measure column (optional; count rows when absent)
 6822:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 6823:   back=<url>    URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 6824: 
 6825: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6826: 
 6827: =head4 INPUT
 6828: 
 6829:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 6830:   cat     string  Required.  X-axis categorical column name.
 6831:   series  string  Required.  Stack-segment categorical column name.
 6832:   val     string  Optional.  Numeric measure column; omit for count mode.
 6833:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 6834:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 6835: 
 6836: =head4 OUTPUT
 6837: 
 6838:   200 text/html    Rendered stacked bar chart page.
 6839:   400 text/plain   Missing cat= or series= parameter.
 6840:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 6841:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 6842: 
 6843: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6844: 
 6845:   stacked_view == lambda self .
 6846:     pre  length(cat_col) > 0 /\ length(series_col) > 0
 6847:          /\ cat_col in columns /\ series_col in columns
 6848:     let rows = _grouped_totals(records, cat_col, series_col, val_col) in
 6849:     post render(stacked, rows)
 6850: 
 6851: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6852: 
 6853:   GET /stacked?l=table:sales&cat=region&series=product&val=amount
 6854:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 stacked bar chart SVG

 6879: =head4 OUTPUT

 6890:          /\ cat_col in columns /\ bar_col in columns /\ line_col in columns
 6891:     post render(combo, pairs)
 6892: 
 6893: =head3 EXAMPLE

 6900: =head2 treemap_view
 6901: 
 6902: C<GET /treemap> -- Render a D3.js treemap where tile area encodes a numeric column.
 6903: 
 6904: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 6905: 
 6906:   group=<col>  Tile-label categorical column (required; cat= is a backwards-compat alias)
 6907:   size=<col>   Tile-area numeric column (required; val= is a backwards-compat alias)
 6908:   topn=N       Keep only the top-N tiles by size; remainder collapsed into "Other"
 6909:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 6910:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 6911: 
 6912: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6913: 
 6914: =head4 INPUT
 6915: 
 6916:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 6917:   group   string  Required.  Tile-label categorical column (cat= also accepted).
 6918:   size    string  Required.  Tile-area numeric column (val= also accepted).
 6919:   topn    int     Optional.  Keep top-N tiles; 0 or absent means no limit.
 6920:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 6921:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 6922: 
 6923: =head4 OUTPUT
 6924: 
 6925:   200 text/html    Rendered treemap page.
 6926:   400 text/plain   Missing group= or size= parameter.
 6927:   400 text/plain   Unrecognised scheme= parameter (?color= returns 400).
 6928:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 6929:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 6930: 
 6931: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6932: 
 6933:   treemap_view == lambda self .
 6934:     pre  length(group_col) > 0 /\ length(size_col) > 0
 6935:          /\ group_col in columns /\ size_col in columns
 6936:          /\ color param absent (scheme= is the correct param name)
 6937:     let tiles = _grouped_totals(records, group_col, [], size_sum) in
 6938:     let top   = topn > 0 ? top_n(tiles, topn) : tiles in
 6939:     post render(treemap, top)
 6940: 
 6941: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6942: 
 6943:   GET /treemap?l=table:sales&group=product&size=amount&topn=10
 6944:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 treemap SVG
 6945: 
 6946: =cut
 6947: 
 6948: =head2 histogram_view
 6949: 
 6950: C<GET /histogram> -- Render a D3.js histogram with server-side binning.
 6951: 
 6952: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 6953: 
 6954:   col=<col>   Numeric column to bin (required)
 6955:   bins=N      Number of bins (default 10; max 200)
 6956:   back=<url>  URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 6957: 
 6958: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6959: 
 6960: =head4 INPUT
 6961: 
 6962:   l     spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 6963:   col   string  Required.  Numeric column to histogram.
 6964:   bins  int     Optional.  Bin count 1-200 (default 10).
 6965:   back  url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 6966: 
 6967: =head4 OUTPUT
 6968: 
 6969:   200 text/html    Rendered histogram page.
 6970:   400 text/plain   Missing col= parameter.
 6971:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 6972:   200 text/html    Empty histogram page when no numeric values exist.
 6973:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 6974: 
 6975: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6976: 
 6977:   histogram_view == lambda self .
 6978:     pre  length(col) > 0 /\ col in columns
 6979:          /\ 1 <= bins <= 200
 6980:     let vals  = { row[col] | row in records, looks_like_number(row[col]) } in
 6981:     let width = (max(vals) - min(vals)) / bins in
 6982:     let bdata = bin(vals, width) in
 6983:     post render(histogram, bdata, bins => |bdata|)
 6984: 
 6985: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6986: 
 6987:   GET /histogram?l=table:sales&col=amount&bins=20
 6988:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 histogram SVG
 6989: 
 6990: =cut
 6991: 
 6992: =head2 calc_list / calc_save / calc_delete
 6993: 
 6994: C<GET /calc/list>, C<POST /calc/save>, C<POST /calc/delete> -- Manage stored
 6995: calculated fields for a table spec.
 6996: 
 6997: Calculated fields are named expression strings evaluated per-row by
 6998: C<Database::BI::Calc> and appended as virtual columns before any aggregation.
 6999: 
 7000: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7001: 
 7002: =head4 calc_list (GET /calc/list)
 7003: 
 7004:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7005:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "fields": { "<name>": "<expr>", ... } }
 7006:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7007: 
 7008: =head4 calc_save (POST /calc/save)
 7009: 
 7010:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7011:           name=<id>  (required; must match /\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z/)
 7012:           expr=<expression>  (required; max 512 chars; hostile patterns rejected)
 7013:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 7014:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }  (compile error or hostile input)
 7015: 
 7016: =head4 calc_delete (POST /calc/delete)
 7017: 
 7018:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7019:           name=<id>  (required)
 7020:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7021:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7022: 
 7023: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7024: 
 7025:   POST /calc/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&name=margin&expr=amount-cost
 7026:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 7027:   GET /calc/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 7028:     -> 200 { "fields": { "margin": "amount-cost" } }
 7029: 
 7030: =cut
 7031: 
 7032: =head2 groups_list / groups_save / groups_delete
 7033: 
 7034: C<GET /groups/list>, C<POST /groups/save>, C<POST /groups/delete> -- Manage
 7035: named value groups that produce virtual C<${col}_group> columns.
 7036: 
 7037: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7038: 
 7039: =head4 groups_list (GET /groups/list)
 7040: 
 7041:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required), col=<col>  (required)
 7042:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "groups": { "<label>": ["<val>", ...], ... } }
 7043:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" } or "col= required"
 7044: 
 7045: =head4 groups_save (POST /groups/save)
 7046: 
 7047:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>, values=<newline-separated values>
 7048:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 7049:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }
 7050: 
 7051: =head4 groups_delete (POST /groups/delete)
 7052: 
 7053:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>  (omit group= to delete all groups for col)
 7054:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7055:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7056: 
 7057: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7058: 
 7059:   POST /groups/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region&group=North&values=MN%0AWI%0AIA
 7060:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 7061:   GET /groups/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region
 7062:     -> 200 { "groups": { "North": ["MN","WI","IA"] } }
 7063: 
 7064: =cut
 7065: 
 7066: =head2 dates_list / dates_save / dates_delete
 7067: 
 7068: C<GET /dates/list>, C<POST /dates/save>, C<POST /dates/delete> -- Manage stored
 7069: date-normalisation patterns that convert non-ISO date columns to ISO-8601
 7070: (C<YYYY-MM-DD>) in-place before aggregation.
 7071: 
 7072: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7073: 
 7074: =head4 dates_list (GET /dates/list)
 7075: 
 7076:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required)
 7077:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "patterns": { "<col>": "<pattern>", ... } }
 7078:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7079: 
 7080: =head4 dates_save (POST /dates/save)
 7081: 
 7082:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, pattern=<fmt>
 7083:           Supported patterns: %m/%d/%Y  %d/%m/%Y  %d-%b-%Y
 7084:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "ok": 1 }
 7085:           400 application/json  { "error": "Unsupported date format pattern ..." }
 7086: 
 7087: =head4 dates_delete (POST /dates/delete)
 7088: 
 7089:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>  (omit col= to delete all patterns for the spec)
 7090:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7091:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7092: 
 7093: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7094: 
 7095:   POST /dates/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=sale_date&pattern=%m/%d/%Y
 7096:     -> 200 { "ok": 1 }
 7097:   GET /dates/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 7098:     -> 200 { "patterns": { "sale_date": "%m/%d/%Y" } }
 7099: 
 7100: =cut
 7101: 
 7102: =head1 DESCRIPTION
 7103: 
 7104: All user-facing routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.
 7105: See the individual action POD above for per-endpoint documentation.
 7106: 
 7107: =head2 Filter operators and the quick-filter bar
 7108: 
 7109: Filters are expressed as C<f=col:op:val> query parameters and are applied
 7110: server-side after all joins.  Multiple C<f=> params are applied in order
 7111: (AND semantics).
 7112: 
 7113: The colon separator is split with a limit of 3, so values may themselves
 7114: contain colons (e.g. C<f=sale_date:eq:2025-01-15>, or a time value like
 7115: C<f=start_time:eq:14:30:00>).
 7116: 
 7117: B<Supported operators:>
 7118: 
 7119:   eq        case-insensitive string equality
 7120:   ne        case-insensitive string inequality
 7121:   contains  case-insensitive substring match
 7122:   starts    case-insensitive prefix match
 7123:   lt        numeric less-than
 7124:   le        numeric less-than-or-equal
 7125:   gt        numeric greater-than
 7126:   ge        numeric greater-than-or-equal
 7127:   empty     cell is undef or empty string (val ignored)
 7128:   notempty  cell is defined and non-empty (val ignored)
 7129: 
 7130: B<Quick-filter bar (table view):>
 7131: 
 7132: A filter row below the toolbar lets the user pick a column, operator, and
 7133: value and press "Apply filter" (or Enter) to add a condition without
 7134: opening any panel.  The current URL is updated with the new C<f=> param
 7135: and the page reloads; all other URL parameters (joins, dedup, etc.) are
 7136: preserved automatically via the browser C<URL> API.
 7137: 
 7138: Active filter chips appear in the toolbar.  Each chip has an individual
 7139: x-button to remove just that condition.  The "x Clear filters" link
 7140: removes all C<f=> params at once.
 7141: 
 7142: B<Drill-down from pie chart:>
 7143: 
 7144: Clicking a pie slice or legend entry on C</pie> navigates to the table
 7145: view with C<f=cat_col:eq:label> appended, so the user can inspect the
 7146: rows behind any slice without manually typing a filter.
 7147: 
 7148: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
 7149: 
 7150: These are the most common mistakes when working with this controller.
 7151: 
 7152: =over 4
 7153: 
 7154: =item B<SQLite files must use the .sql extension, not .sqlite>
 7155: 
 7156: C<Database::Abstraction> (the data-reading library) probes for a file called
 7157: C<tablename.sql> when it wants to open a SQLite database.  It does B<not>
 7158: look for C<.sqlite> or C<.db3>.  If your file is called C<inventory.sqlite>,
 7159: rename it to C<inventory.sql> or it will not appear in the file browser and
 7160: will return 404 when opened.
 7161: 
 7162: =item B<Filter values that contain a colon still work>
 7163: 
 7164: A date like C<2025-01-15> contains hyphens, not colons, so it is fine.  But
 7165: if your value itself contains a colon (for example, a time like C<14:30:00>),
 7166: the filter still works because the C<col:op:val> spec is split on the B<first
 7167: two> colons only -- the rest of the string becomes the value.
 7168: 
 7169:   # This correctly matches "14:30:00" in the start_time column:
 7170:   ?f=start_time:eq:14:30:00
 7171: 
 7172: =item B<Left join keeps only the FIRST matching right-table row>
 7173: 
 7174: When the right table has two rows with the same join key, only the first one
 7175: (in file order) is used.  The second is silently ignored.  If you need all
 7176: matches, consider pre-processing your data so join keys are unique.
 7177: 
 7178: =item B<Export format comes from the filename extension, not a Content-Type header>
 7179: 
 7180: When using C<POST /export> to save a file to disk, the format (CSV or SQLite)
 7181: is determined by the extension of the C<filename> parameter.  C<.csv> produces
 7182: a CSV file; C<.sql> produces a SQLite database.  Any other extension returns
 7183: HTTP 415 (Unsupported Media Type).  The C<Content-Type> request header is
 7184: ignored entirely.
 7185: 
 7186: =item B<Template Toolkit variables starting with underscore are silently dropped>
 7187: 
 7188: If you add a stash variable with a name starting with C<_> (for example,
 7189: C<_tmp> or C<_result>), Template Toolkit will silently produce an empty string
 7190: when the template tries to read it.  This is a TT quirk when C<TRIM =E<gt> 1>
 7191: is active.  Always use names that start with a letter.
 7192: 
 7193: =item B<_apply_filter_spec always passes all records through for unknown operators>
 7194: 
 7195: If you pass an operator that is not in the supported list (for example C<regex>
 7196: or C<like>), the filter is treated as a no-op and B<all rows are returned>.  No
 7197: error is produced.  This is intentional so that future operators can be added
 7198: without breaking existing clients that read a wider response.
 7199: 
 7200: =item B<Uploaded files are evicted automatically, but not immediately>
 7201: 
 7202: Files uploaded via C<POST /upload> are stored in C<.uploads/> under the
 7203: application home directory.  C<Database::BI> evicts upload subdirectories
 7204: whose modification time is older than 24 hours B<on every server startup>.
 7205: Between restarts, files accumulate.  For an immediate full purge (regardless
 7206: of age), post to C<POST /uploads/clear> or click the "Clear upload cache"
 7207: button in the UI.
 7208: 
 7209: =item B<Open C<data/> tables by name; open other files by absolute path>
 7210: 
 7211: The C<view> action (C<GET /view/:table>) only looks inside C<data_dir>.
 7212: To open a file from anywhere else on the filesystem, use C<open_file>
 7213: (C<GET /open?path=/abs/path>).  The two routes use different URL schemes and
 7214: are not interchangeable.
 7215: 
 7216: =back
 7217: 
 7218: =head1 LIMITATIONS
 7219: 
 7220: =over 4
 7221: 
 7222: =item *
 7223: 
 7224: Multi-table joins are delegated to C<Database::Join> with C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>.
 7225: For datasets up to C<join_max_rows> combined rows (default 10,000; configurable
 7226: in C<database_bi.conf>) the join runs in Perl memory; larger datasets spill to
 7227: a temporary SQLite database so peak RAM is bounded by the result set rather than
 7228: the sum of all source tables.
 7229: 
 7230: =item *
 7231: 
 7232: C<_resolve_language> extracts only the primary language subtag from the first
 7233: C<Accept-Language> tag (e.g. C<de> from C<de-DE,de;q=0.9,en;q=0.8>).
 7234: Quality weights and multiple alternatives are not ranked.
 7235: 
 7236: =item *
 7237: 
 7238: No GeoIP-based language resolution is implemented.  The language is resolved
 7239: from the HTTP C<Accept-Language> header only.
 7240: 
 7241: =item *
 7242: 
 7243: C<Sub::Protected> enforcement requires the CHECK compilation phase; when modules
 7244: are loaded dynamically at test time the "Too late to run CHECK block" warning
 7245: is emitted and the restriction is not enforced in that context.
 7246: 
 7247: =back
